# Signal channel knowledge draft

## Root causes

- The pipeline persists a transcript before enqueueing, then treats an existing
  idempotency key as a completed duplicate. Enqueue failure can therefore leave
  a recorded message without a retryable turn. The draft spec includes a focused
  handoff repair (`src/pipeline/message-pipeline.ts:140`,
  `src/pipeline/message-pipeline.ts:212`).

## Quirks

- A direct availability probe successfully invoked `gpt-5.6-luna` with `max`
  reasoning and returned `GPT-5.6-Luna probe OK`. It is usable in this session
  despite its absence from the advertised model list. The approved WDD worker
  configuration remains unchanged; this probe is not implementation or review
  evidence.
- The connector callback currently returns no typed pipeline outcome; the
  shared interface accepts a void-returning handler
  (`src/channels/channel-types.ts:143`). Durable receive acknowledgement cannot
  be inferred from completion of that callback.
- The source-based Signal research is in `research_notes/Signal channel
  feasibility/`. No live account or device test was performed during that
  research. Personal-account operator commands and agent echoes still require
  live provenance verification.
- Pinned v0.14.9 listDevices JSON omits the current-device marker that its
  standalone CLI prints. Setup must establish device metadata explicitly;
  runtime must not invent isThisDevice in the RPC response
  (`research/signal-cli-v0.14.9/src/main/java/org/asamk/signal/commands/ListDevicesCommand.java:47-67`).
- The bridge uploads a long-text attachment above 2,000 UTF-8 bytes. Signal
  text-only replies must be chunked by bytes, not JavaScript string length
  (`research/signal-cli-v0.14.9/lib/src/main/java/org/asamk/signal/manager/config/ServiceConfig.java:24`,
  `research/signal-cli-v0.14.9/lib/src/main/java/org/asamk/signal/manager/internal/ManagerImpl.java:947-954`).
- Send failures can preserve timestamp/results under error.data.response;
  challenge tokens in those results must not reach logs or agent errors
  (`research/signal-cli-v0.14.9/src/main/java/org/asamk/signal/jsonrpc/SignalJsonRpcCommandHandler.java:249-290`).

## Decisions

- Ivo agreed to the immutable epic name `signal-channel` and to use the existing
  draft spec as the intake source.
- Ivo approved retaining the current WDD constitution/configuration and
  inheriting all settings for this epic on 2026-10-14. Those approvals were
  recorded through wddctl; no epic overrides were added.
- Ivo chose both personal-linked and dedicated Signal account modes, DMs and
  groups, and deferred attachments while cross-channel attachment work proceeds
  separately.
- Ivo chose both Note to Self and selected chats as personal-account entry
  points: Note to Self activates without a prefix; selected chats require an
  explicit trigger. Agent output never activates another turn.
- The canonical spec is `.wdd/epics/signal-channel/spec.md`, copied byte-for-byte
  from `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-signal-channel-spec.md`. Ivo explicitly
  signed off on that text on 2026-10-14; wddctl recorded all 15 acceptance
  criteria, including Unix-socket transport, disappearing-message exclusion,
  and focused handoff repair. No adversarial review was requested first.
- Contract research was recorded by Codex using the v0.14.9 source snapshot,
  contract inventory and immutable knowledge input. Design approval was subsequently recorded following Ivo's implementation
  instruction on 2026-10-15. No live validation is recorded.

## Review catches

- Claude Opus 5.5's externally requested general+security review found one P1
  and seventeen P2s; original findings are immutable in
  research/spec-review-1.md. Coordinator dispositions accept every finding.
  Closure means a specified design remedy, pending independent confirmation
  and human sign-off; no implementation or live evidence is implied.
- F1: agent-runner send failures retry the entire queue turn, including earlier
  sends/tools. Stream and queue catches also erase typed errors
  (src/daemon/agent-runner.ts:895; src/queue/queue-processor.ts:173).
  Revised design uses a terminal run error, tool failure latch and a durable
  queue-payload marker before Signal use, so wrapping/crash recovery cannot
  redispatch that provider turn. Recovery requires an explicit new command.
- F2/F3/F4/F15: receive account aliases come from listAccounts' number/ACI pair;
  first-connect retry belongs to the connector. The draft now defines every
  verification/receive-exception transition and exactly supported v0.14.9.
- F5: membership does not establish the current bridge device after relinking.
  The draft requires personal bridge creation metadata and a strictly newest
  linked-device rule, plus verified frozen human-device rosters across instances.
  Newer/tied/unknown device creation fails closed. This operational constraint
  remains a proposal and a mandatory live-evidence gate.
- F6/F7: personal output becomes final-only except intentional tool/completion
  messages; showToolCalls=true is refused. An account gate serializes sends,
  persists rate/challenge pauses and needs explicit local challenge recovery.
- F8–F14: the revised design specifies config/wire encodings, missing-target
  refusal, bounded framing/buffer recovery, metadata-free aggregate ignored
  diagnostics, strict parse-error sanitization, private same-UID socket trust
  and synthetic identity/timestamp rules for evidence.
- F16: transactional rollback alone does not replay input. The draft adds an
  optional Result-aware receipt and a bounded connector FIFO/backoff actor.
  Stop/crash/overflow/deadline losses remain before durable acceptance.
- F17/F18: CLAUDE.md joins required documentation; the deliverable script must
  execute all listed focused surfaces, including AC-1/AC-12 and new runner,
  queue, receipt, account-state and local recovery checks.

- Round 2: Claude Opus 5.5's second external general+security review upholds all
  eighteen original findings/dispositions and reports R2-F1/R2-F2 as P1 plus
  five P2s. The immutable report is research/spec-review-2.md. The coordinator
  accepts all seven; closure is in the revised design, awaiting independent
  confirmation and human convergence. The following refinements supersede
  round 1's broad terminal-send/receive-exception/recovery policies.
- R2-F1: default queue concurrency is five. Rejecting a busy account while
  making every NOT_SENT terminal loses ordinary replies. Revised design uses
  a bounded FIFO and saved final/background text/chunk cursors in queue payload,
  with delivery-only defer distinct from provider maxAttempts. A delivery retry
  never queries the provider or repeats earlier tools. Partial/unknown current
  chunks stay terminal. No new queue status or generic transport redesign.
- R2-F2: JsonMessageEnvelope has a normal UntrustedIdentityException branch;
  a remote contact's per-message error is not account-level failure evidence
  (pinned JsonMessageEnvelope.java:34-45). Exclude that notification only,
  including invalid event fields; keep other receiving active. No raw error
  classifier, verification amplification or protocol-loop counter for it.
- R2-F3/R2-F4: drain active send on reload, then separate an unknown delivery
  from account recovery using an automatic fence. Only real server challenges
  require explicit resume. A handled Signal tool error permits an explanation
  on its origin channel; operation/account safeguards do not silence Telegram
  or a healthy Signal destination.
- R2-F5: background notification delivery branches before provider setup
  (src/daemon/agent-runner.ts:66-69) and has no run ID. Guard/checkpoint identity
  must use queue item/delivery ID, with real run ID optional. Notification text,
  task/status and nullable outbound run ID survive delivery-only recovery; no
  fabricated provider execution/session.
- R2-F6/R2-F7: listDevices is a server call, so refresh gets one persisted
  account budget and cached proof rather than per-send calls. Transient refresh
  failure preserves the healthy subscription. Local listAccounts separately
  refreshes number/ACI aliases; unknown aliases have bounded quarantine and
  cannot be trusted from sender text. Runtime selection uses pinned ACI.

- Round 3 upheld the round-1/2 remedies and accepted convergence after four
  P2 clarifications: bounded stale-proof quarantine; durable per-destination
  leases for contiguous chunks; purge cancellation with no late resurrection;
  and a target-specific rather than account-wide invocation latch.
- Pre-commit independent review caught explicit/progress leases without a
  durable retry actor. Such leases now release on every invocation return, and
  startup removes pre-reservation orphan leases after reservation classification.
- Ivo instructed implementation and completion of all tasks on 2026-10-15.
  This authorizes in-scope planning/execution; target-branch merge remains
  human-owned and AC-15 still requires real-device evidence.

## TASK-001 implementation decisions — initial worker report

These are the initial captured choices, not a clean-review claim. Independent
pre-commit review identified acceptance/count invariant defects in D4 and D10;
the revised entries and verified closure below supersede those initial claims.

- **TASK-001 D1 — Shared schema integration:** `SignalConfigSchema` owns Signal defaults/normalization. Existing named `ChannelConfigSchema` transforms Signal config only, preserves other connector defaults, and reports safe config field paths. `parseSignalConfig` returns a generic safe `ConfigError` Result. Filesystem trust checks remain the later connection seam; this schema checks absolute/NUL-free socket syntax only.
- **TASK-001 D2 — Selector consistency:** UUID selectors normalize to lowercase and must equal the pinned UUID when statically comparable. E.164 selectors remain bootstrap hints for later verified account resolution. Authorization lists deduplicate after normalization; no phone/name authorization keys.
- **TASK-001 D3 — Canonical identity APIs:** UUID config/wire codecs normalize case; persisted external IDs must already be lowercase canonical v1 IDs. Standard padded Base64 and unpadded Base64url have separate strict round-trip codecs; decoded destinations retain standard Base64 for later RPC use. Optional expected-account checking returns safe ACCOUNT_MISMATCH.
- **TASK-001 D4 — Error identity/retry:** `SignalDeliveryError` extends `ChannelError`, retaining its CHANNEL_ERROR code for generic compatibility and a distinct class plus typed fields. Deferred/cancelled/non-retryable errors have distinct codes; wrappers retain `deliveryError` directly, never a raw cause. Invalid acceptance metadata fails closed to UNKNOWN; partial/unknown/complete current chunks cannot request retry, while proven-unsent current chunks may defer despite earlier complete chunks.
- **TASK-001 D5 — Optional receipt contract:** `onMessageWithReceipt` uses string statuses accepted/duplicate/dropped in a sync-or-async neverthrow Result. Ordinary `onMessage` is untouched; nothing is wired here.
- **TASK-001 D6 — Trusted output contract:** `AgentOutput.trustedDelivery` contains queueItemId, deliveryId, optional real runId and purpose reply/background/explicit/progress. This is host-only metadata, not a model argument or wire field. Background checkpoints reject runId rather than fabricating one.
- **TASK-001 D7 — Prepared delivery/receipt contract:** synchronous `prepare(destination, output)` returns a typed normalized destination and readonly fixed chunk strings; async `sendPreparedChunk(destination, chunk, trustedContext)` returns only complete acceptance plus safe success/failure counts. No remote identities/timestamps/tokens are exposed in receipts.
- **TASK-001 D8 — Proof snapshots:** readonly local unverified/verified account snapshots carry generation, configured bridge-device identity, proof age, and a readonly human-device roster with creation timestamps. They deliberately do not mirror invented listDevices current-device/registration fields; publication and validation are later tasks.
- **TASK-001 D9 — Persisted contract spelling:** reserved keys are `__talond_signal_delivery_v1` and `__talond_signal_delivery_started_v1`. `nextChunk` is the first unsent index and equals acceptedChunks. Phases are ready/in_flight/delivered/terminal; in_flight uses unknown acceptance. The invocation marker is separately typed/parsed, not a saved reply or model input.
- **TASK-001 D10 — Fail-closed checkpoint parsing:** `parseSignalDeliveryCheckpoint(input, {queueItemId, now})` accepts JSON text or a persisted object, rejects extra fields/inconsistent identity/cursor/outcomes, enforces 1MiB body and combined chunk text, 2,000-byte nonempty well-formed UTF-8 chunks, 3MiB serialized size, and exact 24-hour lifetime. Returned checkpoint/chunks are frozen. Pending expired work errors; delivered/terminal records remain parseable after expiry for local finalization/inspection without sending. No body reconstruction, provider replay or runtime transitions occur here.

### TASK-001 first pre-commit review

- **P1 accepted:** error metadata allowed NOT_SENT/refused with zero earlier accepted chunks but a positive known success count to retain delivery-only retry. Contradictory acceptance evidence must become UNKNOWN/unknown/none while retaining safe counts.
- **P2 accepted:** terminal checkpoint validation admitted PARTIAL/refused with no earlier accepted chunk, and current partial success without enough total successes for earlier complete chunks plus the current one. Persisted state must reject those contradictions.
- Parent verified both findings against the actual source. The original worker fixed them with separate regression RED/GREEN evidence before a second fresh independent review.

### TASK-001 revised decisions and review closure

- **TASK-001 D4 (revised) — Error identity/retry:** `SignalDeliveryError` remains a distinct `ChannelError` subclass with CHANNEL_ERROR compatibility, safe typed fields and no raw remote cause. Deferred/cancelled/non-retryable errors preserve their prior identities and typed deliveryError reference. In addition to enum/safe-integer validation, outcome/current-acceptance/count relationships must be consistent: successes cover prior complete chunks; partial/complete current acceptance needs at least one additional success; proven-unsent current acceptance with no prior chunk cannot contain known successes. NOT_SENT has no known acceptance; PARTIAL has consistent known acceptance; UNKNOWN is current unknown. Contradictions fail closed to UNKNOWN/unknown/none while retaining every individually safe known count and safe hint. Group success totals are not forced equal to chunk totals. Consistent proven-unsent current chunks may still defer after prior complete chunks; accepted/partial/unknown current chunks never become retryable.
- **TASK-001 D10 (revised) — Fail-closed checkpoint parsing:** Existing queue-bound parsing, strict fields, canonical destination/account checks, frozen outputs, 1MiB body/combined chunk bounds, 2,000-byte well-formed nonempty chunks, 3MiB serialized limit and fixed 24-hour lifetime are unchanged. Pending expiry errors while delivered/terminal records remain available for local finalization/inspection. Terminal PARTIAL now requires either an unsent current chunk with at least one prior complete chunk (and existing success-count lower bounds), or current partial acceptance with total successes strictly greater than prior complete-chunk count. This rejects the reviewed impossible terminal states without equating group-recipient totals to chunk counts. No downstream execution/recovery behavior is added.
- D1–D3 and D5–D9 are unchanged; no new implementation decisions were reported.
- Fresh independent GPT-6.1-sol xhigh follow-up found no remaining critical/high/medium findings. The controller independently ran changed-source lint, all 375 focused tests, build, CLI help and the serialized fake terminal smoke successfully. Hashes of all thirteen reviewed files were unchanged after verification, with no staged files and no remaining smoke listener.
- TASK-001 committed that exact content as `0c2dc55b4d485aaa264e670ce8311fed1dba145a`. A fresh exact-commit WDD review was clean; the controller re-ran the complete focused/lint/build/startup gate at that commit, recorded passed verification and current freshness, and integrated it locally into `wdd/signal-channel` at `ae8c6c0ebbed4e041c96150fc98557b479a08573`.

## Confirmed WDD execution constraints

- WDD v0.4.2 `submit` on the `pr` surface calls `push_branch` before attempting PR creation. A branch-reference fallback does not mean publication was skipped. After the unauthorized first task push was disclosed, Ivo approved local-only integration. The current effective `merge.surface` is `local`; no further push/PR creation is authorized and the already-pushed branch stays untouched.
- This version's `start` ignores the configured task branch pattern; supply the approved `--branch task/signal-TASK-NNN` and absolute repo-local `--worktree` explicitly. Do not adopt existing generic task branches from another epic.
- Its integration worktree path is hard-coded as a sibling of the supplied repository. The controller keeps integration inside the approved `.worktrees/` root by supplying the absolute controller `--state` and TASK-001 worktree as `--repo` on the first local merge. The resulting base checkout is `.worktrees/SCOPE-signal-channel/TASK-001.wdd/integration/SCOPE-signal-channel`; later merges can target this clean base checkout with the same explicit controller state. Workers never run these commands.
- Nested worktrees resolve the ancestor checkout's `node_modules` without installation. A root `node_modules` symlink is seen as untracked because the existing ignore pattern has a trailing slash; no symlink is needed.
- The full-repository lint baseline has 75 errors and 21 warnings before Signal source edits; task-owned changed-source lint and the build pass. Report the unrelated baseline rather than changing it or claiming global lint is clean.

## TASK-002 implementation decisions

Captured from the initial worker report; fresh pre-commit review was clean. The
controller independently passed changed-source lint, 50 focused tests, build,
CLI help and serialized fake terminal smoke, with all five reviewed hashes
unchanged. Only that content is authorized for commit.

1. **TASK-002 D1:** Reuse the public synchronous repository transaction runner through a small generic callback-runner helper, rather than exposing the protected database, adding another connection or changing bootstrap/constructor injection. Real SQLite rollback tests verify this existing seam.
2. **TASK-002 D2:** Place thread creation and dedup inside the transaction along with transcript/routing/enqueue. Apply the atomic fix to legacy pipeline calls too, so ordinary connectors cannot retain the same new-failure gap.
3. **TASK-002 D3:** Use a per-invocation private Error sentinel, distinguish it by identity, return the original Err Result, and let unexpected exceptions propagate to the existing pipeline Result boundary. Error rather than Symbol also satisfies the project's throw-error lint rule.
4. **TASK-002 D4:** Use the synchronous Result callback signature plus runtime validation. Reject native async functions before invocation; reject Promise/non-Result returns before commit. This is not an async transaction API; callers must not schedule detached writes.
5. **TASK-002 D5:** Name the additional method `handleInboundEventWithReceipt`; map the existing outcomes through a private narrowed union without changing `pipeline-types.ts`, shared TASK-001 receipt types or legacy signatures. TASK-012 must bind this method when installing optional receipt handlers.
6. **TASK-002 D6:** Count processed attempts once in the common entry path and duplicate/no-persona counters only after transaction success. Errors are counted once outside the transaction. Preserve snapshot stats semantics.
7. **TASK-002 D7:** Keep normalized data locally Readonly and never mutate the incoming event. Test frozen events and unchanged serialized input rather than modifying shared normalizer/type files outside task ownership.
8. **TASK-002 D8:** For Signal, replace unsafe failure causes/messages with fixed stage reasons and one safe `LOCAL_ACCEPTANCE_FAILED` diagnostic (configured channel name only). Also remove remote/key fields from Signal thread/debug/drop diagnostics. For non-Signal errors, preserve typed Error causes and expose stage/cause text; exact old human-readable wording is not retained.
9. **TASK-002 D9:** Preserve historical duplicate behavior: no backfill/repair queue for old transcript-only rows. Preserve intentional no-persona transcript/drop auditing and classify its replay as duplicate.
10. **TASK-002 D10:** Use actual migrated in-memory SQLite and SQL triggers for the primary regression, not a mocked acceptance API. Additional spies inject faults only at existing production method boundaries; enqueue-after-write injection still runs the actual QueueManager/QueueRepository. Legacy orchestration mocks get only the existing `transaction` runner method.
11. **TASK-002 D11:** Keep scope limited to the five owned paths. No README/setup/architecture edits or receipt factory/bootstrap wiring; broader epic documentation/integration remain controller/later-task responsibilities.

## TASK-003 implementation decisions — initial report

These are the initial captured choices, not a clean-review claim. Fresh review
found a verified P2 in D14: cancelling pending requests can free slots while Node
retains socket-buffered writes under backpressure. The original writer's revised
D14/new D23–D24 and verified review closure below supersede that initial claim.

1. **TASK-003 D1:** Use Node's built-in `net.Socket`, Buffer, TextDecoder, crypto UUIDs, fs lstatSync and the existing Zod/neverthrow dependencies; no new dependency or generic transport abstraction.
2. **TASK-003 D2:** Use a dormant `SignalRpcClient` class with one connection attempt, explicit `connect/request/stop`, synchronous receive callback, and fixed-code close callback. Later lifecycle owns retries, backoff, failure-loop counting, verification and wiring.
3. **TASK-003 D3:** Return merged `SignalDeliveryError` subclasses for transport failures to preserve identity and retry/outcome metadata rather than introducing a competing delivery error contract.
4. **TASK-003 D4:** Keep remote application errors distinct from transport failures: safe local enum plus sanitized send evidence; TASK-007 decides recipient/chunk/rate outcomes. Error envelopes never become successful RPC results.
5. **TASK-003 D5:** Return opaque immutable untrusted JSON only for non-send method successes; normalize send success and `error.data.response` through the same evidence parser before returning to callers.
6. **TASK-003 D6:** Retain timestamp validity only, not actual bridge timestamps, in send evidence. Retain challenge presence only, never challenge values; strip all remote error messages, unknown error data, and recipient identities from errors/evidence.
7. **TASK-003 D7:** Preserve malformed recipient entries, unknown result types, invalid delays and missing/invalid timestamp as incomplete evidence. Do not filter failures away or default missing state to success. A parseable evidence set may contain explicit failure types; complete is not accepted.
8. **TASK-003 D8:** Validate all three serialized nullable recipient-address fields and canonical optional group IDs before calling an entry structurally valid, then discard those identities. This uses supplied pinned sources rather than invented mock shapes.
9. **TASK-003 D9:** Mirror Java signed 32-bit subscription IDs (including zero and wraparound-negative values) through a shared helper, rather than assuming unsigned IDs.
10. **TASK-003 D10:** Generate fresh UUID string request IDs, never coerce incoming numeric/null IDs, and guard every socket callback by its connection object. Stale callbacks cannot settle another connection's pending work.
11. **TASK-003 D11:** Use one fixed 1 MiB byte framing buffer per connection, processing incoming chunks incrementally and parsing only complete lines. No partial-line resynchronization after failure.
12. **TASK-003 D12:** Count original retained pre-response frame bytes against 1 MiB; keep frozen notifications in a private append-only-until-release queue. Match the confirmed subscription before callback delivery; discard the early queue on close/failure/stop.
13. **TASK-003 D13:** Freeze parsed JSON iteratively rather than recursively; use child-by-child pushes so valid wide/deep bounded JSON is not rejected by JavaScript argument/call-stack limits.
14. **TASK-003 D14:** Submit each accepted RPC write immediately with no unbounded outgoing queue. Request capacity refusal, disconnected state and pre-abort are definitely unwritten. After entering socket.write, false returns and throws remain potentially written. Never retry inside transport.
15. **TASK-003 D15:** A cancelled/timed-out possibly accepted subscribe closes its socket because its unknown subscription ID cannot safely be left as a hidden receive handler. Ordinary pending-request cancellation does not cancel unrelated calls or a healthy subscription.
16. **TASK-003 D16:** Strictly whitelist the reviewed runtime request methods and parameter subsets, rejecting extra fields. Enforce the merged 2000-byte text cap here as defense against implicit media; formatting/chunking remains TASK-007.
17. **TASK-003 D17:** Reuse the normalized config timing envelope: positive integer request deadlines 1000–120000 ms. The final timeout test uses the real 1000 ms minimum instead of a sub-config-bound test-only deadline.
18. **TASK-003 D18:** Use synchronous read-only lstat traversal immediately before/after connect, capturing full-path bigint inode/device/UID/mode snapshots. Reject noncanonical traversal spellings instead of resolving away a potentially symlinked component.
19. **TASK-003 D19:** Return transient DISCONNECTED only for a missing final socket under fully validated ancestry on the initial check. Missing/replaced paths during the post-connect comparison are UNTRUSTED_SOCKET.
20. **TASK-003 D20:** Stop is immediate transport cancellation, not lifecycle drain. Future owner must drain and persist first; submitted requests settled by stop remain UNKNOWN and are not replayed.
21. **TASK-003 D21:** Real test fixtures live only in generated private HOME directories; fixture-only chmod/rename/removal is allowed and cleaned up. Foreign UID checks use narrowly substituted lstat results, avoiding chown/operator mutations. /tmp ancestry is explicitly rejected.
22. **TASK-003 D22:** No documentation/shared export-barrel/runtime factory changes: exact conflict-domain ownership and dormant-until-TASK-012 instructions take precedence over expanding this task into integration.

## TASK-004 implementation decisions

Fresh pre-commit review found no qualifying defects. The controller independently
passed changed-source lint, all 59 focused tests, build, CLI help and serialized
fake terminal smoke, with all seven reviewed hashes unchanged. The worker
honestly disclosed that initial registry helper code preceded its first RED;
policy/actor/diagnostic RED/GREEN evidence is recorded separately.

1. **TASK-004 D1:** Separate immutable normalized candidates from admitted pipeline events so revalidation can repeat authorization without retaining an upstream envelope or attachment fields.
2. **TASK-004 D2:** Expose normalization and policy as two Result-returning functions; the buffer composes them and returns original typed policy errors rather than erasing identity in wrappers.
3. **TASK-004 D3:** Keep socket `connectionGeneration` distinct from the generation-wide registry generation; verify it and the active subscription before touching receive content. Lifecycle must supply the current verified connection context.
4. **TASK-004 D4:** Hash an ordered JSON tuple of version/account/canonical conversation/author/original source device/data timestamp with SHA-256. This avoids delimiter ambiguity, preserves identity across wrappers and channel renames, and includes no local receive time.
5. **TASK-004 D5:** Use the merged TASK-001 proof/registry and InboundReceiptHandler contracts directly. No speculative duplicate shared contract or durable repository was introduced.
6. **TASK-004 D6:** Put generation-wide advisory refresh coalescing in the registry (60 seconds per owned personal ACI). The injected owner remains responsible for persisted scheduler start writes, one in-flight RPC, startup/reconnect budget and server pauses. This task never calls listDevices or grants proof from a refresh request.
7. **TASK-004 D7:** Use one bounded receive actor with three independent immutable-array queues and one earliest-deadline wake timer, rather than three public actors whose handoffs could bypass authorization. The capacities/deadlines/counters remain independent.
8. **TASK-004 D8:** Count encoded UTF-8 candidate bytes for quarantine and encoded candidate-plus-frozen-event bytes for receipt FIFO. The latter conservatively accounts for retained text/provenance twice; queues never exceed 1 MiB encoded retention.
9. **TASK-004 D9:** On successful alias refresh, re-evaluate all held candidates exactly once and drop still-unrelated aliases. On timeout/error, retain only until the original deadline and retry at most once per five seconds; socket/device proof is not reset.
10. **TASK-004 D10:** After alias ownership was verified, receipt/proof revalidation uses the pinned canonical account ACI, not a now-obsolete phone alias. Number changes cannot revoke the already established original account provenance, while config/account replacement still fails closed.
11. **TASK-004 D11:** Preserve the exact admitted event object and dedup key through receipt retries; current config/proof is checked separately and never regenerates delivery text/key. Receipt outcomes accepted/duplicate/dropped all release the FIFO head through the existing Result contract.
12. **TASK-004 D12:** Distinguish proof-wait wakeups from receipt failures. Revalidation may wake a proof waiter but cannot reset database-error backoff into a hot retry loop.
13. **TASK-004 D13:** Continue bounded deadlines during a pending async receipt, but never invoke a second handler concurrently. Expired/stopped entries cannot be resurrected by late results. The existing handler API has no cancellation signal, so an already executing transaction itself cannot be recalled.
14. **TASK-004 D14:** Use fixed diagnostic reason unions, an allowlisted reason check and payloads limited to channel/counts. No raw logger/error/schema-path adapter is added. Separate diagnostics.stop() cancels its own aggregate timer; lifecycle must stop both owned components.
15. **TASK-004 D15:** Reset loss-warning episodes on recovered capacity/new retention after an empty expired queue, retain cumulative per-actor dropped counts, and coalesce receipt-overflow reconnect notification until capacity recovers.
16. **TASK-004 D16:** Treat missing/nonzero/unverifiable expiration state conservatively as excluded; never default an absent timer to zero. Preserve full Note to Self text; selected-chat trigger stripping follows the approved case/whitespace rules.
17. **TASK-004 D17:** Leave lifecycle wiring, actual server calls, durable state, production transaction integration, documentation and terminal smoke to their designated tasks/controller, as the explicit conflict domains require.

### Reconciliation after the first parallel producer reports

- Read both queued notes, all TASK-002/003/004 Decisions, the shared contract inventory, and unstarted TASK-008/012 briefs. The same-connection receipt and lifecycle dependency obligations already match the approved design and existing consumer briefs; no approved brief/context bytes need changing.
- TASK-012 must bind the concrete `handleInboundEventWithReceipt` method. TASK-008 owns the real transport/policy glue: transport emits flattened `SignalReceiveNotification { subscription, account, envelope?, hasException }`, while `SignalInboundBuffer.receive` accepts manual `{ subscription, result: { account, envelope, exception? } }` params plus the verified connection generation. Handle `hasException` as a fixed exclusion before policy, without inventing or retaining an exception body; map the safe nonexception fields deliberately and test the actual producer callback through the buffer. Do not assume the two producer API shapes are interchangeable.
- Preserve one shared generation registry, persisted server-refresh budget, real receipt handler, config/proof revalidation and stop of both buffer/diagnostics. No local receipt acknowledges upstream acceptance, and an already executing receipt handler cannot be cancelled retroactively.
- These are consumer handoff facts derived from the actual producer APIs, not scope/design changes or completion evidence. The producer reviews and local integration outcomes are recorded below.

### TASK-002 and TASK-004 local integration outcomes

- TASK-002 exact reviewed commit `360db0c740a2edafc88bed11ce420fd5402735ba` was independently checked for correct parent, exact five-path scope and all committed blob hashes. Fresh exact-commit xhigh review had no findings; the controller re-ran changed-source lint, all 50 focused tests, build, CLI help and serialized fake smoke at that commit, then recorded current freshness and locally integrated it as `2dbadbd07478509b7b1913afead8f623febadeb8`.
- TASK-004 exact reviewed commit `06ad5a9f764a26a3813c04fd12c88a085b81ace8` had matching seven-path/blob identity, clean independent exact-commit review, and controller-repeated 59-test/lint/build/CLI/fake-smoke gate. Freshness was nonmaterially stale after TASK-002 because owned domains do not overlap; WDD locally integrated it as `311fc4f3006fd76852f5cd7e2693a3d167e9aa3a`.

### TASK-003 backpressure correction decisions and review closure

- **TASK-003 D14 (revised):** Submit a request only if both the pending pool and the connection's outgoing-write gate have capacity. Mark a write as submitted before entering socket.write, preserving UNKNOWN for a false-returning or throwing write. A false return latches connection backpressure until actual drain, irrespective of request cancellation/timeout/settlement. Refuse later requests before writing with transient definite-unwritten CAPACITY. The actual outgoing writable-buffer invariant is at most the socket high-water mark plus one bounded encoded request frame (1 MiB + newline), not merely at most 64 pending RPC entries. No automatic retry or application outgoing queue.
- **TASK-003 D23 (new):** Keep one drain listener and flag on each connection. Clear capacity only when the listener's captured connection is still current. Remove that listener and reset its flag immediately on stop/close; stale captured callbacks cannot affect replacement connections. This makes byte-capacity ownership independent of pending-request ownership without changing exported APIs or later lifecycle policy.
- **TASK-003 D24 (new):** Reproduce buffering with actual paused private HOME Unix peers and a source-permitted large lookup. Use pass-through socket observation and fake RPC deadline advancement, not mocked backpressure returns, for repeated abort/timeout tests; use actual peer resume/drain for recovery. Separately invoke an old captured drain callback to verify replacement isolation after listener cleanup. This targets production request behavior and the accepted P2 without adding services or mutating operator paths.
- Decisions 1–13 and 15–22 are unchanged. A fresh independent xhigh follow-up found no remaining critical/high/medium findings. Controller independently passed changed-source lint, isolated strict test types, all 60 focused tests, build, CLI help and serialized fake terminal smoke; all five candidate hashes remained unchanged.
- The retained writer committed only that content as `18d9251d0de275142049e1a0e4130691ba6e35f1`. Fresh exact-commit WDD review was clean; parent independently verified its exact parent/path/blob identities and repeated the complete committed gate before WDD recorded passed verification/nonmaterial freshness and locally integrated it at `2632f00db0ea7e376d40e021cd13b3a4f690519f`.

### TASK-005 controller decision — privileged verification budget

- The ordinary device-check scheduler correctly refuses challenge/rate/recovery pauses. The approved `signal-resume` flow nevertheless requires verified account/device state before clearing a challenge; clearing first or a general scheduler bypass would violate that flow.
- The worker is authorized to expose a distinct narrowly named challenge-resume check-start repository method, not an ambient bypass flag on ordinary checks. It may ignore only the challenge bit for that one verification reservation while enforcing the same expected-revision CAS, persisted shared sixty-second check budget and unexpired rate/recovery deadlines. It does not clear any pause, permit sends, publish proof or execute network work.
- TASK-008/011/012 must restrict that method to authenticated operator IPC with an explicit external-resolution assertion. Account/device verification must succeed before clearing challenge via the check generation/revision CAS; an intervening/new challenge or state change invalidates stale verification. Do not refresh the revision and blindly retry clearing. Ordinary startup/reconnect/background/send paths keep their pause refusal.
- Repository tests must prove unchanged challenge/restrictions, shared budget, preserved deadlines and stale/repeated-call refusal. The actual implementation and full flow remain subject to their assigned worker/reviewer gates; this entry records the approved-flow plumbing decision, not execution evidence.

## TASK-005 implementation decisions — initial review candidate

Captured from the worker's READY_FOR_REVIEW report. Fresh independent pre-commit
and exact-commit reviews found no critical/high/medium findings. Parent verified
all seven committed blob hashes and repeated changed-source lint, all 51 focused
tests, build, CLI help and serialized fake terminal smoke at the exact commit.
WDD integrated `d0b65ff92e4a8f92714ee7f5df902675987eaf04` locally into
`wdd/signal-channel` at `3bb9a9bab448220d66979f4517f87427b4448e6e`.

1. **TASK-005 D1:** Used the actual TASK-001 canonical identity helpers and `TrustedDeliveryContext`/purpose union instead of defining duplicate identity or purpose contracts. Persisted keys normalize ACI casing; external IDs must already be canonical and belong to that account.
2. **TASK-005 D2:** Used readonly camelCase domain snapshots over private snake_case SQL rows. Snapshots, nested reservations and returned reservation arrays are frozen; no caller object is mutated.
3. **TASK-005 D3:** Used a per-account revision increment for every successful state mutation; idempotent `getOrCreate` does not change state/revision. A stale owner/result cannot overwrite newer state. SQLite transaction serialization protects the read/check/write sequence.
4. **TASK-005 D4:** Nullable last-start values distinguish no previous budget use from a valid epoch-zero start. Explicit safe integer `now` arguments make persisted scheduling independent of BaseRepository's process-local timestamp counter; valid next-attempt arithmetic is checked before budget writes.
5. **TASK-005 D5:** Persisted separate rate/recovery deadlines plus a manual challenge bit, deriving effective pause kind/deadline in SQLite. This avoids losing a committed timed fence when resolving challenge. Timed causes may remain recorded after expiry; expiry is not an automatic row mutation.
6. **TASK-005 D6:** Classified rate input uses an absolute not-before deadline. Enforced a 15-minute minimum without a downward cap and a 60-second recovery minimum. Fence detection uses max(now, persisted updatedAt), conservatively preventing clock rewind from shortening a newly detected fence. Wire retry-delay/challenge parsing belongs to transport/gate consumers.
7. **TASK-005 D7:** Kept account reservation queue identity deliberately non-FK while checking row existence before reservation. Purge can cascade logical-message leases without deleting uncertainty about an already submitted RPC. Classification after purge never recreates queue data.
8. **TASK-005 D8:** Used a fresh random lease generation on every acquisition, in addition to queue/delivery/kind owner CAS. This prevents a late release from deleting a reacquired lease even with identical timestamps and delivery identity.
9. **TASK-005 D9:** Same-owner lease acquisition is idempotent and does not renew timestamps; no expiry policy is embedded in the repository. Saved checkpoint lifetime/terminal classification remain actor responsibilities.
10. **TASK-005 D10:** Used the existing shared synchronous Result transaction helper for every multi-write mutation. Consumers must compose using the same DB connection; no callback that performs network is introduced.
11. **TASK-005 D11:** Added local `SignalStateError` with fixed enum reasons and optional safe scheduling hint, extending existing DbError while dropping raw SQLite messages/causes. No existing Signal delivery/checkpoint error was wrapped or erased.
12. **TASK-005 D12:** Orphan cleanup deletes only explicit/progress leases, requires no unresolved reservation and the expected account revision, and is explicitly startup/reload-only with dispatch quiesced. It never replays providers or rewrites queue payloads/status; saved reply/background leases require their own checkpoint classification.
13. **TASK-005 D13:** Added ONLY the supervisor-approved explicit privileged challenge-resume device-check operation. Ordinary checks/sends still refuse challenge. The exact authorization and unchanged successful-check revision contract is above; later callers own its enforcement.
14. **TASK-005 D14:** Production WAL/FK connection factory is used in disposable file-backed fixtures, including real competing connections and close/reopen. Only fixture-owned temporary SQLite directories are removed. No real account/service is accessed.
15. **TASK-005 D15:** Migration runner tests upgrade an actual version-013 schema with existing queued work, assert version 014/idempotence and SQL constraints. No old migration, runner source, queue policy, constructor wiring or other task implementation changed.
16. **TASK-005 D16:** No external wire-shaped field/method/result was implemented here. This task consumes local identity/context/checkpoint/error contracts; no mock was used to infer RPC shapes, no moving upstream source was consulted, and no source-only test is presented as live evidence.
17. **TASK-005 D17:** Documentation/integration/lifecycle changes were not made because this task explicitly owns only the seven persistence paths. Later wiring/docs tasks must describe the new tables and consumer obligations.

### TASK-005 merged concrete consumer contracts

- `SignalAccountStateRepository`: `findByAccount`, `getOrCreate`, `findReserved`, `startDeviceCheck`, `startChallengeResumeDeviceCheck`, `reserveSend`, `clearReservation`, `recordPause`, `clearChallenge`. Every successful state mutation advances revision; returned reservation/revision must be used in later classification CAS. Pause expiry uses the time/deadline, not a reset enum.
- `SignalDestinationLeaseRepository`: `find`, `acquire`, `validateOwner`, `release`, `releaseOrphanInvocations`; expected owner includes the fresh `leaseId` generation. Same-owner acquire is idempotent without lifetime renewal.
- Compose operations through the same SQLite connection and `runResultTransaction(fn => repo.transaction(fn), operation)` with checkpoint expected-state writes. Returned Err rolls back nested mutations. A purged queue row must not be recreated/finalized when its retained account reservation is classified; orphan invocation cleanup requires dispatch quiesced and reservations already classified.
- These are reviewed merged producer API facts, not a completed downstream flow. No new/revised worker Decisions beyond the seventeen entries above were reported.
- The combined first-five-task integration passed all fourteen focused files (595 tests), ESLint for twenty-two changed source files, build, CLI help and serialized fake terminal smoke. The integration worktree stayed clean; no smoke listener remained.

### TASK-006 execution partition and handoff reconciliation

- The approved task scope spans independently testable persistence, dispatch and startup boundaries. Execution is partitioned into sequential exclusive owners in the same WDD-owned worktree, with no overlapping active writer and no intermediate commits: A owns queue repository/checkpoint primitives and repository tests; B owns processor/manager/retry/dead-letter/types/mapper and their tests; C owns only lifecycle/barrel/recovery-entry tests after both producer handoffs. Each has a real host-run lint/focused-test/build/help gate. A whole-task fresh xhigh review precedes any commit.
- Preserve every component's Decisions and hash-pinned uncommitted handoff. B/C cannot silently edit a producer's files; a needed producer fix returns to that owner. This changes neither the sixteen-task plan nor any approved brief/context bytes.
- Existing `QueueProcessor` catch currently discards Error identity, and lifecycle broadly resets claimed/processing rows using a private-db cast. TASK-006 owns those boundaries. `AgentQueryAttemptError` is private in `agent-runner.ts` and also drops causes; TASK-010 explicitly owns the provider/query/final-send wrapper fix. Do not broaden TASK-006 into that file or claim complete runner non-replay proof before the later task.
- TASK-005 supplies the same-connection transactional account/lease primitives, reservation survival after purge, revision/lease-generation CAS and classified orphan cleanup. Actor consumers must preserve those ownership identities and quiesced sequencing. The privileged challenge-resume API remains exclusive to the later authenticated verification flow; it is not a generic scheduler or send bypass.

### TASK-006 Component A uncommitted persistence handoff

All four reported hashes were independently verified; parent source lint and the real repository plus unchanged checkpoint suites passed **103 tests in two files**. The component's own lint/47-test/build/help gate also passed. B is the current exclusive writer; C and whole-task independent review remain pending. These are candidate interfaces, not a merged TASK-006 contract or full runner/live proof.

Component A report: `cad2d556-8c1d-4ad0-afc8-590fb9101196/TASK-006/persistence-handoff.md` in native output artifacts. Preserve all reported Decisions:

- **componentA-D001 — Reuse producer contracts.** New shared exports are state/ownership/settlement/recovery compositions only. Reuse TASK-001 checkpoint/guard/trusted-purpose/error contracts and TASK-005 account/lease/pause/reservation types; no duplicate wire contract, schema migration, or external bridge field.
- **componentA-D002 — Strong expected-state CAS.** Compare the complete canonical checkpoint snapshot (therefore phase/cursor plus identity/text/counts), not only a stale payload snapshot. Uses synchronous SQLite Result transactions and safe latest-row merges. Real phase/cursor and competing-connection conflicts are tested.
- **componentA-D003 — Atomic pre-write boundary.** Lease acquisition, checkpoint in-flight, invocation guard, account reservation and send spacing commit together; failed revision/ownership/SQL/nested Err rolls everything back. No account/lease producer changes required.
- **componentA-D004 — Fresh revision, exact reservation generation.** Settlement rereads account revision because metadata checks can advance it during a send; verifies exact queue/delivery/purpose/start token before clearing. A regression first failed, then passed with the fix.
- **componentA-D005 — Purged callback classification.** Classify the already-submitted account operation without reviving the missing queue row; return DeliveryCancelled after committing internal classification. Normal enclosing Result rollback remains deliberate and documented. No body/history callback executes for missing rows.
- **componentA-D006 — Compatible classification-only expiry extension.** TASK-001 parser gained optional `allowExpired`; default behavior is unchanged. It is needed to classify interrupted/expired persisted state and settle a submitted chunk after deadline. Begin/ready-defer never use it to authorize network work. Delivered local writes remain recoverable after expiry.
- **componentA-D007 — Separate local/delivery retry.** Defer preserves provider attempts/maxAttempts and saved 24-hour expiry, clamps independent caller backoff, and supports delivered local-write retry without provider/RPC replay or a past-deadline hot loop. No delivery retry-count schema added.
- **componentA-D008 — Safe unfinished-run transitions.** Purge/recovery/terminal writes update only queue-linked pending/running runs, use fixed local reasons, and bound ended_at safely under clock rewind/future starts. Completed runs and background tasks remain unchanged. Existing purge selector/return contracts are retained.
- **componentA-D009 — Reservation-first orphan recovery.** Quiesced recovery classifies all reservations before orphan lease cleanup. A lease acquired before reservation also guards its owning execution against provider replay; merely deleting that lease was insufficient and had a failing real-repository regression before correction.
- **componentA-D010 — Approved legacy distinction.** Escalated malformed generic payload compatibility; supervisor approved read/recovery-only ordinary fallback, strict mutations, conservative escaped/truncated detection, and independent ownership precedence. Parsed root objects use own-key presence only. Malformed/non-object bytes normalize complete Unicode escapes and recognize `__talond_sig` or longer reserved prefixes. This is conservative taint detection, not partial-JSON repair. Completely unrecognizable corruption without any remaining reserved-prefix/ownership evidence cannot be reconstructed; legitimate writes are SQLite-atomic. Legacy bytes remain untouched and valid incidental key-name text does not trip the guard.
- **componentA-D011 — No payload repair in mutations.** Save/guard/checkpoint transitions require strict root objects, even where inspection preserves old ordinary behavior; never silently replace legacy content with `{}`. Persist terminal classification before clearing the last independent ownership evidence on damaged Signal work.
- **componentA-D012 — Checked original run identity.** Validate queue/thread/persona association on saved checkpoint reads/writes; allow finished runs only for delivered/terminal inspection and standalone guard classification. This prevents persisted foreign-run IDs reaching local finalization. Real foreign-run regression passed after first failing.
- **componentA-D013 — Guarded local finalization callback.** Expose a same-connection synchronous callback transaction rather than invent outbound history keys/background mutations or require downstream access to private db. Callback identity-preserving Err rollback and completed-delivery idempotence are tested.
- **componentA-D014 — Live dispatch rejection is separate from quiesced recovery.** `rejectSignalReplay` handles invalid/guard-only rows without a global recovery mutation. It rereads current state and refuses to clobber newly saved valid checkpoints. Stale callbacks/terminal state cannot reopen delivery.
- **componentA-D015 — Narrow dormant handoff.** No dispatch loop, quiesce manager, lifecycle, runner, CLI, connector factory, docs, or other tests edited. Account waiting/preflight/registry/capability/binding validation, B catch identity/slot handling, C startup/reload wiring, TASK-010 AgentQueryAttemptError identity preservation and finalization integration remain other owners' work. No claim of full runner/provider-count or real-device proof.

### TASK-006 Component B supervisor-approved dispatch boundary

- **componentB-D001 (approved; implementation pending):** `QueueProcessor.processNext` and `QueueManager.startProcessing` may accept an optional separate typed saved-delivery callback, preserving existing single-handler callers. Selection uses the latest repository inspector. Ready/delivered checkpoints never invoke the ordinary callback, even when the new seam is absent or errors; default is bounded delivery-only defer/slot release, with ready expiry terminalization and delivered local-write retry after expiry. Preserve the callback through loop/quiesce/resume. Unexpected active in-flight/terminal states stay blocked for quiesced recovery, not global recovery inside a live processor. Re-inspect state after callbacks so an Ok/Err cannot falsely complete unsent work or revive cancelled/terminal work. A's replay rejection is only for invalid/guard-only state and refuses stale overwrites of valid checkpoints. TASK-010 supplies the saved actor boundary; TASK-012 wires it without routing through provider execution. Current tests can prove actual queue callback selection, not complete AgentRunner/provider integration.
- **componentB-D002 (approved; implementation pending):** A current live run that started ordinary, added only an explicit/progress guard and returned Ok after a handled tool outcome may complete its ordinary origin explanation. This is explicitly allowed by the design, not permission to replay a pre-existing guard. Err/throw after Signal use remains terminal; any post-handler checkpoint overrides the callback result; reply/background guard-only state is not this exception. Queue cannot infer target health/permission from the guard. Preserve unrelated public missing-ID behavior (`complete` remains idempotent Ok; plain generic `fail` remains QueueError), while the new strict Signal-aware dispatch/settlement path preserves permanent DeliveryCancelled. Do not rewrite baseline generic expectations to justify a broader API change. Public completion cannot bypass saved checkpoint finalization, which requires A's same-connection callback transaction.
- **componentB-D003 (approved; implementation pending):** Queue retries only queue-owned Result inspection/defer/reject/complete/fail transitions. Actor/callback owns retained generated text/RPC receipts and retries of its save/settlement writes; a durable ready/delivered checkpoint may safely hand off to delivery/local-only retry. A leaked Signal-specific state/checkpoint failure without safe saved state is a contract violation: fixed safe warning, blocked claim, slot release, no generic provider retry, no invented receipt/closure/schema. Do not classify every generic DB/queue error this way or spin on non-DATABASE conflicts. The fallback is not a durable new guard: design explicitly retains the pre-checkpoint crash boundary when no checkpoint/guard/independent ownership committed. Existing durable evidence still prohibits replay. TASK-009/010 must prove the retained-output/receipt write-only retry contract; TASK-012 wires it.
- **componentB-D012 supervisor disposition / producer correction:** B found that Signal defer/actor deadlines use supplied wall time, but existing `findPending` default and both atomic claim methods compare retries against process-wide `BaseRepository.now()`. That counter advances even on reads and remains inflated after clock rewind; a saved delivery can therefore become immediately eligible again. A discovery-only B filter is insufficient: the subsequent atomic claim can select another future-deferred row, including ahead of collaboration work. Do not accept this as the feature's residual limitation or hide it by resetting the clock in the reproducing case. Preserve ordering timestamps, BaseRepository and unrelated generic clock semantics. Between-test use of the existing test reset API is allowed, but dedicated real-repository/dispatcher regressions retain the future counter/rewind and prove no early claim/hot loop. No simultaneous writer, new schema, commit or approved-input change is authorized.

Same-protocol continuation `b9b6f1bd-6eee-4f01-a2fd-e35baa339459` resumes retained A for the narrow fix, then retained B to complete/strengthen tests, then integration-only C and fresh whole-task review. No Task-006 completion/commit is implied.

B checkpoint report: `cad2d556-8c1d-4ad0-afc8-590fb9101196/TASK-006/dispatch-handoff.md`. All reported Decisions:

- **componentB-D001 — Supervisor-approved optional typed saved-dispatch seam.** Preserve existing one-handler callers while preventing a saved checkpoint from reaching that handler. Missing saved callback defers rather than executing provider work. Canonical latest inspector state selects dispatch. Post-handler state overrides callback outcome.
- **componentB-D002 — Supervisor-approved live marker-Ok exception and legacy boundaries.** Pre-dispatch invocation guards always reject. An ordinary execution that began without guard/checkpoint may add an explicit/progress guard and return Ok after handling a tool outcome; this can complete its ordinary queue item. Any Err/throw after invocation rejects, and reply/background guard without checkpoint rejects even on Ok. Queue does not infer healthy-origin target eligibility; TASK-009/010 owns that. Generic complete(unknown) remains Ok and fail(unknown, generic) remains QueueError, retaining their original tests. Known saved dispatch/missing-row settlement is strict cancellation instead; the public optional savedDispatch argument carries this context on fail.
- **componentB-D003 — Supervisor-approved queue-owned transition retries only.** Private retry loop encloses only queue-owned synchronous Result inspection/state transitions, never handler/actor invocation. Database transition failures back off; non-DATABASE conflicts do not loop. A leaked SignalStateError/SignalCheckpointError without safe saved state is returned unchanged, logged only with fixed safe text, and leaves its claim blocked while releasing the slot. This is a callback contract violation, not permission to rerun provider. An in-memory blocked claim is NOT a durable replay guard: the approved pre-checkpoint crash boundary remains if no checkpoint/marker/independent ownership ever committed.
- **componentB-D004 — Independent bounded delivery delay without a new counter/schema.** Use retryStrategy attempt zero for saved delivery/local-finalization deferrals, clamped to 1–60 seconds. Preserve A's absolute later account hint and ready-expiry cap; never use/increment provider attempts or renew checkpoint lifetime. No delivery retry counter or retained process map was added. This timing guarantee is currently blocked by D012's repository eligibility defect.
- **componentB-D005 — Claim quiesce is not service lifecycle.** Synchronous quiesceClaims/resumeClaims toggles new claims only; active handlers keep running, optional callbacks remain captured, and resume never starts a stopped interval/services. Slot count remains guarded by existing try/finally. No runner cancellation/drain/reload implementation is inferred.
- **componentB-D006 — Capped local database backoff.** Queue-owned state transitions retry at 1–60 seconds using the configured retry function and a bounded exponent. They can retain an active slot while their state decision is uncertain; other slots remain available. Persistent database outage has no invented elapsed cutoff/provider replay. The callback itself is invoked once. A real SQLite trigger regression proves deferred-write rollback/retry without callback repetition.
- **componentB-D007 — Identity-preserving queue catches.** A thrown Error instance is passed intact to fail, including DeliveryDeferredError, NonRetryableRunError and DeliveryCancelled; returned Err likewise remains intact. Unknown non-Error throws become a fixed local Error, not String(remoteValue). Typed cancellation returns the exact error, stops queue settlement, and does not retry/recreate work. Signal typed diagnostics never enter generic raw-message retry logging.
- **componentB-D008 — No duplicate interrupted-send recovery.** Live processor never calls global recovery or guesses settlement for in_flight/terminal state; it leaves that claim out of polling and releases the concurrency slot. A.rejectSignalReplay handles only invalid/standalone guard boundaries and rereads to avoid clobbering a newly valid saved checkpoint. Only A primitives defer, terminate and inspect saved work.
- **componentB-D009 — Public saved completion must be guarded.** Direct complete refuses saved checkpoints; successful actor Ok is not a substitute for A.finalizeSignalDelivery. Completed/dead-letter decisions cannot be overwritten by late handler completion/failure. Missing saved work is cancellation; no row recreation or in-memory final body append is added.
- **componentB-D010 — Reuse destination/order semantics and existing dead-letter producer.** A's same-thread saved head block and TASK-005 lease ownership remain authoritative. Queue tests verify collaboration bypass while the saved lease remains owned. Existing DeadLetterHandler handles ordinary exhaustion and immediate typed nonretryable ordinary failure with fixed safe reasons; no new lease, dead-letter state machine, or SQL was added in B. dead-letter.ts, queue-mapper.ts and retry-strategy.ts remain untouched because their implementations did not need changing for this scoped seam.
- **componentB-D011 — Honest real queue-layer tests.** Real migrated SQLite plus real QueueRepository/Processor/Manager transitions, not a test-only runner, exercise repeated deferrals, maxAttempts=3/concurrencyLimit=5, absent/present callbacks, delivered-after-expiry, local finalization, purge/late settlement, reserved input, leases/order, typed errors and original baseline tests. Ordinary callback zero calls is a queue-dispatch assertion, NOT full AgentRunner/provider/tool/network non-replay proof. Some added closure-local expectations are caught by the processor's error boundary; final acceptance review should prefer their outside-callback persisted/result assertions and strengthen actor-side evidence during continuation where needed.
- **componentB-D012 — Stop on producer-owned retry-clock defect.** Dedicated behavioral RED above; supervisor rejected B-side filtering and requested retained A fix, then a new hash-pinned handoff before B resumes. A bytes are unchanged. Test isolation does not reset away the actual regression.

Eligibility of an explanatory origin reply belongs TASK-009/010. Public complete cannot bypass those boundaries. Generic unknown-ID complete/fail expectations remain unchanged. Both explicit/progress and reply/background cases are parametrized separately.
- **componentB-D003 — Write ownership / retained output-receipt / pre-checkpoint boundary (supervisor approved).** Queue retries only its own transitions; actors retain generated output and RPC receipts and retry their writes without replaying work. Leaked Signal errors without safe saved state block rather than create provider retries, but this alone cannot prevent replay after a crash before any durable evidence. No generic state machine, receipt closure or schema pretends otherwise.

- **componentB-D005 — Quiesce is claim control, not service lifecycle.** Synchronous flags pause/resume new claims on the same loop/callbacks. Active work retains its slots until returning. Resume does not start a stopped loop/services. No reload drain/cancellation implementation is inferred.
- **componentB-D006 — Capped queue-owned database retries.** Retry only local Result state work with 1–60-second cap; callback never repeats. State uncertainty may hold a slot during DB outage; no invented maximum elapsed cutoff/provider replay. Actual SQLite trigger rollback tests cover saved defer and nested dead-letter write failure. Signal-safe failure handling does not expose RPC errors/identifiers/text.

#### TASK-007 parent preparation clarification

In response to A run `fce1290d-bb3b-42e8-9e54-461f615ba5ca`, supervisor request `0c6c828f-f41b-427c-948e-09945460c27d`, the controller authorized the existing producer-compatible policy: empty post-Markdown/whitespace-only text or an ill-formed UTF-16 original body is permanent NOT_SENT/current unwritten INVALID_RESPONSE, not a synthesized/repaired chunk. Shared preparation reuses the existing inclusive SIGNAL_CHECKPOINT_TEXT_MAX_BYTES (1MiB) ceilings for original and aggregate formatted text, with OUTPUT_TOO_LARGE on excess, including explicit callers. Attachment rejection takes precedence. This preserves checkpoint/wire admission and avoids an unusable empty chunks vector; no new config/decoder/ID cap is introduced. A must record this uniform preparation bound and all trade-offs for independent review/downstream documentation. Chosen signature: `prepareSignalText(destination, output, accountUuid)` returning the existing typed prepared text/error contract; selection remains C's ownership. This clarification is not a claim that A or the complete sender is implemented or reviewed.

### TASK-007 A text producer handoff

A `fce1290d-bb3b-42e8-9e54-461f615ba5ca` is ready, with only `signal-text.ts` and its test added. Report: native workflow `dde76aa7-6ccf-441d-94d1-bd3971d135b8/TASK-007/text-handoff-A.md`. Worker observed meaningful RED29fail/27pass, then all56pass plus lint/strict source-test tsc/build/help. Parent read both complete files, independently verified both hashes, base/empty index/protected tracked diff, and repeated source lint and **56/56 tests**. No independent whole-task review/commit or runtime/live proof yet. A hashes: source `78c019cab513f6c46c3bf4eed5a94eec6ee0fd7998851e329acb4c1249090cbc`; test `8d2231007dda97b55195ee80584b2557956c8e206162c82425c20d4edbfaa158`.

ALL reported Decisions, preserved without triage:

1. **A-01:** Export only `prepareSignalText(destination, output, accountUuid)`; consume existing types and keep account selection external. The supervisor explicitly approved this signature.
2. **A-02:** Bind account scope using the existing `parseSignalExternalId`, rather than writing a second parser. Preserve its canonical-ID requirement, normalized expected-account behavior, Base64url round trip and account-mismatch precedence. No new ID/decoder size cap.
3. **A-03:** Map only the identity parser's `ACCOUNT_MISMATCH` to that same delivery reason; other parser failures map to `INVALID_DESTINATION`. Never retain the identity error as a raw cause.
4. **A-04:** Reject any nonempty attachment list before reading `body` or parsing the destination. Empty lists are allowed. Rejection does not inspect/retrieve attachment bytes and returns no partial chunks, including for zero-byte attachments.
5. **A-05:** Read the original body after attachment/destination validation; check original byte ceiling before allocating its UTF8 round-trip buffer. Then reject ill-formed original UTF16, including invalid surrogates in Markdown portions that formatting would otherwise remove.
6. **A-06:** Reuse the existing `SIGNAL_CHECKPOINT_TEXT_MAX_BYTES` (inclusive 1,048,576 bytes), not a new constant/configuration option. Apply it to original body and aggregate formatted text before chunk creation, uniformly for saved and explicit/progress preparation. This uniform existing-ceiling policy was explicitly authorized by the supervisor.
7. **A-07:** Return permanent unwritten `OUTPUT_TOO_LARGE` on text overage; never truncate or accept an oversized original merely because formatting would shorten it. The current formatter only removes content, so the formatted-size guard is defensive against future formatter changes; no independent reachable formatted-only-overage input exists with today's helper.
8. **A-08:** Reject ill-formed UTF16 as permanent unwritten `INVALID_RESPONSE`, using the same UTF8 round-trip predicate as the merged checkpoint producer. No Unicode repair/replacement or new error enum. This resolution was explicitly authorized by the supervisor.
9. **A-09:** Call existing `stripMarkdown` once on the complete valid body, not on individual chunks. Preserve that helper's documented/existing trimming, Markdown removal and blank-line collapse; do not promise that raw Markdown is unchanged. The guarantee is exact ordered reconstruction of the single formatted result.
10. **A-10:** Reject empty post-format text (including whitespace/Markdown-only input) as permanent unwritten `INVALID_RESPONSE`. Do not emit empty chunks, fabricate content, or return an empty array that the checkpoint/wire producers reject. This resolution was explicitly authorized by the supervisor.
11. **A-11:** Greedily accumulate JavaScript string-iterator code points using actual UTF8 byte counts, flushing only when the next point would exceed the existing `SIGNAL_MAX_CHUNK_BYTES`. Do not split UTF16 surrogate pairs. Combining sequences/graphemes/words may cross chunk boundaries; grapheme-safe/word-aware splitting was not approved or required.
12. **A-12:** Never trim, reformat, annotate, add separators to, or remove whitespace from individual chunks. Chunk concatenation is exactly the one-pass formatter result; saved chunks can begin/end with whitespace or Markdown-looking text.
13. **A-13:** Freeze the returned aggregate/destination/chunk array, without mutating caller-owned `AgentOutput`, attachments, actions, or trusted metadata. ALL reported Decisions, preserved without triage:

1. **B-01:** Export two pure policy functions and two small readonly interfaces; reuse existing Result/error/receipt/pause/evidence types. Supervisor approved the cumulative API and shared interpreter; no coordinator/queue API is invented.
2. **B-02:** `previous` excludes the current chunk, uses cumulative recipient counts and earlier completely accepted chunks. Keep acceptedChunks unchanged on every Err; return local counts on Ok to match TASK006 settlement semantics.
3. **B-03:** Consume actual TASK003 `kind:'send'` evidence and `SignalRpcRemoteError.sendEvidence`; do not decode JSONRPC or raw send entries a second time. A metadata `kind:'result'` cannot become a send result by reparsing.
4. **B-04:** Only ordinary complete timestamp-valid nonempty entirely valid SUCCESS yields Ok. Require positive valid evidence flags, not address/token/timestamp values discarded by the producer. Receipt denotes bridge acceptance, not recipient delivery/read.
5. **B-05:** Count each valid known SUCCESS or failure entry once. Invalid entries and UNKNOWN types contribute no claimed successes/failures. Valid NETWORK_FAILURE contributes a known failure count while still making acceptance UNKNOWN.
6. **B-06:** Incomplete, invalid timestamp, empty, invalid-entry or unknown-type evidence yields UNKNOWN with valid known counts retained. Any NETWORK_FAILURE takes uncertainty precedence even beside valid successes/refusals.
7. **B-07:** Whole non-network valid refusals use refused current acceptance; distinguish overall NOT_SENT from PARTIAL due only to previous complete chunks. Mixed current successes/failures use terminal PARTIAL/current partial.
8. **B-08:** Every error envelope remains Err. Supervisor explicitly corrected the initial proposed all-success-error classification to UNKNOWN/current unknown, preserving counts, because complete/PARTIAL cannot persist through TASK006. No producer was changed to accommodate B.
9. **B-09:** Bare remote -3/-5/-6 sends are UNKNOWN rather than inferred whole refusals. Independently observed rate/challenge restrictions still survive.
10. **B-10:** Only entirely valid rate-only whole refusals use delivery_only. Any permanent recipient failure in the set disables retry; mixed current success/failure or uncertainty always forbids retry. A challenge may coexist with a resumable proven-refused cursor but still requires external resolution/verified clearance.
11. **B-11:** Reuse the actual transport error's current acceptance, reason and retryScope rather than selecting transient codes independently. Before-write is unwritten; handed-to-socket is unknown. Add cumulative prior counts without wrapping a raw transport cause.
12. **B-12:** Keep the restriction interpreter reusable for read-only remote metadata errors and separate from send uncertainty. Do not give a failed metadata read a send UNKNOWN/recovery classification.
13. **B-13:** Treat RATE_LIMIT code, RATE_LIMIT_FAILURE type or a valid server retry delay as rate evidence. Inspect restrictive sanitized hints even when their entry otherwise fails validation; do not discard restrictive evidence with malformed identities/type.
14. **B-14:** Take max(15-minute minimum, every safe delay deadline); absent/zero delay does not remove the minimum. Never cap a longer server delay downward.
15. **B-15:** Validate both seconds-to-milliseconds multiplication and now-plus-delay addition as nonnegative safe integers. Invalid delay or overflow requires manual challenge; do not emit an unsafe absolute deadline.
16. **B-16:** Preserve safe rate deadlines from other entries under invalid/overflowing delay or challenge. If the 15-minute minimum itself cannot be represented, return challenge instead of a fake bounded deadline.
17. **B-17:** Use only sanitized challengeRequired (including empty-string/non-string token-presence cases retained by TASK003) and normalized CHALLENGE_REQUIRED code. Never inspect, copy or retain token values or remote error text.
18. **B-18:** Return independent hints in rate, challenge, then recovery order, not a single highest-priority pause. This preserves a longer rate delay after challenge clearance. Caller must persist all hints, including hints beside Ok.
19. **B-19:** Append automatic recovery only for send UNKNOWN, using the existing recovery pause representation (repository owns the 60-second deadline). Do not infer a manual challenge from ordinary uncertainty or require operator resume for it.
20. **B-20:** Error notBefore combines safe rate and automatic recovery deadlines only. It does not encode/clear challenge or committed state; caller respects both all new hints and previously committed restrictions.
21. **B-21:** Validate trusted time/progress defensively after classification input arrives, while documenting that the caller must validate before RPC. Unsafe/corrupt inputs cannot yield Ok/retry and are not silently repaired into valid checkpoint state.
22. **B-22:** Guard cumulative count addition and complete-cursor increment. Preserve valid prior lower bounds for overflowing totals and safe sums for others; never emit unsafe counts or invent successes to repair inconsistent prior state.
23. **B-23:** Use existing fixed safe delivery reasons: transport's reason; INVALID_RESPONSE for malformed input; NETWORK_FAILURE for network evidence; RPC_ERROR for contradictory all-success errors; PARTIAL_ACCEPTANCE for valid mixed current results; challenge/rate/recipient refusal precedence for whole refusals. Classification result, not remote text, determines them.
24. **B-24:** Allocate/freeze returned classification, successful receipt, restriction array and entries without mutating input. Existing SignalDeliveryError class supplies safe identity/message/providerRetryable=false and no cause. No logger, DB call, RPC, timer or sender/target/text change exists in B.
25. **B-25:** Test real sanitized producer integration through parseSignalRpcFrame/parseSignalSendEvidence with source-shaped fixtures; also use a minimal direct sanitized fixture to prove no discarded fields are required. Read pinned source before writing conformance fields/assertions.
26. **B-26:** Assert actual codec representability rather than duplicate its schema. Eleven named terminal cases cover both prior-count states, including all-success errors; an additional 420-combination policy matrix checks every non-Ok result against the actual terminal/ready codec, including deferred whole-rate refusals and permanent/non-network/malformed/challenge/unknown branches.
